总序与前言

Introduction
1
总序
中文

总序

中国有一个漫长的自然农业历史，中国的农民至今还占总人口的绝大多数。五千年的中华文明，基本上是农业文明。农业文明的基础是乡村的社会生活。在广阔的乡土社会里，以农民为主，加上小手工业者、在乡知识分子和明末清初从农村兴起的各地商人，一起创造了像海洋般深厚瑰丽的乡土文化。庙堂文化、士大夫文化和市井文化，虽然给乡土文化以巨大的影响，但它们的根扎在乡土文化里。比起庙堂文化、士大夫文化和市井文化，乡土文化是最大多数人创造的文化，为最大多数人服务。它最朴实、最真率、最生活化，因此最富有人情味。

乡土文化依赖于土地，是一种地域性文化，它不像庙堂文化、士大夫文化和市井文化那样有强烈的趋同性，它千变万化，更丰富多彩。乡土文化是中华民族文化遗产中至今还没有被充分开发的宝藏，没有乡土文化的中国文化史是残缺不全的，不研究乡土文化就不能真正了解我们这个民族。

乡土建筑是乡土生活的舞台和物质环境，它也是乡土文化最普遍存在的、信息含量最大的组成部分。它的综合度最高，紧密联系着许多其他乡土文化要素或者甚至是它们重要的载体。不研究乡土建筑就不能完整地认识乡土文化。甚至可以说，乡土建筑研究是乡土文化系统研究的基础。

乡土建筑当然也是中国传统建筑最朴实、最真率、最生活化、最富有人情味的一部分。它们不仅有很高的历史文化的认识价值，对建筑工作者来说，还可能有一些直接的借鉴价值。没有乡土建筑的中国建筑史也是残缺不全的。

乡土生活赋予乡土建筑丰富的文化内涵，我们力求把乡土建筑与乡土生活联系起来研究，因此便是把乡土建筑当做乡土文化的基本部分来研究。聚落的建筑大系统是一个有机整体，我们力求把研究的重点放在聚落的整体上，放在各种建筑与整体的关系以及它们之间的相互关系上，放在聚落整体以及它的各个部分与自然环境和历史环境的关系上。乡土文化不是孤立的，它是庙堂文化、士大夫文化、市井文化的共同基础，和它们都有千丝万缕的关系。乡土生活也不是完全封闭的，它和一个时代整个社会的各个生活领域也都有千丝万缕的关系。

我们力求在这些关系中研究乡土建筑。例如，明代初年"九边"的乡土建筑随军事形势的张弛而变化，江南和晋中的乡土建筑在明代末年随着商品经济的发展所发生的变化历历可见，等等。聚落是在一个比较长的时期里趋向定形的，这个发展过程蕴含着丰富的历史文化内容，我们也希望有足够的资料可以让我们对聚落作动态研究。总之，我们的研究方法综合了建筑学、历史学、民俗学、社会学、文化人类学的各种方法。方法的综合性是由乡土固有的复杂性和外部联系的多方位性决定的。

从一个系列化的研究来说，我们希望选作研究课题的聚落在各个层次上都有类型性的变化：有纯农业村，也有从农业向商业、手工业转化的村；有窑洞村，也有雕梁画栋的村；有山村，也有海滨村；有马头墙参差的，也有吊脚楼错落的；还有不同地区不同民族的；等等。这样才能一步步接近中国乡土建筑的全貌，虽然这个路程非常漫长。在区分乡土聚落在各个层次上的类别和选择典型的时候，我们使用了细致的比较法，就是要找出各个聚落的特征性因子，这些因子相互之间要有可比性，要在聚落内部有本质性，要在类型之间或类型内部有普遍性。

因为我们的研究是抢救性的，所以我们不选已经闻名天下的聚落作研究课题，而去发掘一些默默无闻但很有价值的聚落。这样的选题很难：聚落要发育得成熟一些，建筑类型比较完全，建筑质量好，有家谱、碑铭之类的文献资料。当然聚落还得保存得相当完整，老的没有太大的损坏，新的又没有太多。但是，近半个世纪来许多极精致的或者极有典型性的村子都已经被破坏，而且我们选择的自由度很小，有经费原因，有交通原因，甚至还会遇到一些有意的阻挠。我们只能尽心竭力而已。

因为是丛书，我们尽量避免各本之间的重复，很注意每本的特色。特色首先来自聚落本身，在选题的时候，我们加意留心它们的特色，在研究过程中，我们再加深发掘。其次来自我们的写法，不仅尽可能选取不同的角度和重点，甚至变换文字的体裁风格。有些一般性的概括，我们放在某一本书里，其他几本里就不再反复多写。至于究竟在哪一本书里写，还要看各种条件。条件之一，虽然并不是主要条件，便是篇幅。

有一些已经屡屡见于过去的民居调查报告或者研究论文里的描述、分析、议论，例如"因地制宜"、"就地取材"之类，大多读者早就很熟悉，我们便不再啰嗦。我们追求的是写出每个聚落的特殊性，而不是去把它纳入一般化的模子里。只有写题材的特殊性，才能多少写出一点点中国乡土建筑的丰富性和多样性。所以，挖掘题材的特殊性，是我们着手研究的切入点，必须下比较大的工夫。对类型性特殊性和个体性特殊性的挖掘，也都要靠细致运用比较的方法。

这套丛书里每一本的写作时间都很短，因为我们不敢在一个题材里多耽搁，怕的是这里花工夫精雕细刻，那里已拆毁了多少个极有价值的村子。为了和拆毁比速度，我们只好贪快贪多，抢一个是一个，好在调查研究永远只能嫌少而不会嫌多。工作有点浅简，但我们还是认真地做了工作的，我们绝不草率从事。

虽然我们只能从汪洋大海中取得小小一勺水，但这勺水毕竟带着海洋的全部滋味。希望我们的这套丛书能够引起读者们对乡土建筑的兴趣，有更多的人乐于也来研究它们，进而能有选择地保护其中最有价值的一部分，使它们免于彻底地毁灭。

陈志华

1998年春

English

General Preface

China has a long history of natural agriculture; Chinese farmers still account for the vast majority of the total population. Five thousand years of Chinese civilization is basically agricultural civilization. The foundation of agricultural civilization is rural social life.

In vast vernacular society, mainly farmers, plus small handicraftsmen, rural intellectuals, and merchants emerging from rural areas in late Ming-early Qing, together created vernacular culture as profound and magnificent as the ocean.

temple culture, scholar-official culture, and marketplace culture, although exerting enormous influence on vernacular culture, have their roots in vernacular culture. Compared to temple culture, scholar-official culture, and marketplace culture, vernacular culture is created by and serves the vast majority.

It is most plain, most sincere, most生活化, therefore most full of human warmth. vernacular culture depends on land, is a regional culture; unlike temple culture, scholar-official culture, marketplace culture with strong convergence, it is ever-changing, more rich and colorful.

vernacular culture is a treasure in Chinese cultural heritage not yet fully developed; Chinese cultural history without vernacular culture is incomplete; not studying vernacular culture cannot truly understand our nation.

vernacular architecture is the stage and material environment of vernacular life; it is also the most ubiquitous, most information-rich component of vernacular culture. It has highest comprehensiveness, closely linked to many other vernacular cultural elements or even serves as their important carrier.

Not studying vernacular architecture cannot fully understand vernacular culture. Even can say, vernacular architecture research is the foundation of vernacular culture system research.

vernacular architecture is of course also the most plain, most sincere, most生活化, most human-warm part of Chinese traditional architecture. They not only have high historical-cultural cognitive value, for architects, may also have some direct reference value. Chinese architectural history without vernacular architecture is also incomplete.

Vernacular life endows vernacular architecture with rich cultural connotation; we strive to research vernacular architecture in connection with vernacular life, thus researching vernacular architecture as basic part of vernacular culture.

settlement building macro-system is an organic whole; we strive to put research focus on settlement entirety, on relationships between various buildings and whole and their mutual relationships, on relationships between settlement entirety and its various parts with natural and historical environments.

vernacular culture not isolated; it is common foundation of temple culture, scholar-official culture, marketplace culture, with countless connections to them. Vernacular life also not completely closed; it has countless connections with all life domains of an era's entire society. We strive to research vernacular architecture in these relationships.

E.g., early Ming "Nine Frontiers" vernacular architecture changed with military situation tension-relaxation; Jiangnan and central Shanxi vernacular architecture changes with commodity economy development in late Ming clearly visible, etc.

settlements tend to finalize in a relatively long period; this development process contains rich historical-cultural content; we also hope enough materials allow dynamic research of settlements. In short, our research method integrates architecture, history, folklore, sociology, cultural anthropology various methods.

Method comprehensiveness determined by inherent complexity of vernacular and multi-directional external connections.

From a serialized research perspective, we hope selected research settlements have typological changes at all levels: pure agricultural villages, also villages transforming from agriculture to commerce-handicraft; cave-dwelling villages, also carved-beam-painted villages; mountain villages, also seaside villages; horse-head-wall varied, also stilt-house varied; also different regions different ethnic groups; etc.

Thus can step by step approach full picture of Chinese vernacular architecture, although this journey very long. In distinguishing vernacular settlement categories at various levels and selecting typical ones, we use detailed comparative method, i.e.

find characteristic factors of each settlement; these factors must be comparable, essential within settlement, universal between or within types.

Because our research is rescue-oriented, we do not select world-famous settlements as research topics, but discover unknown but valuable settlements. Such topic selection difficult: settlement should be maturely developed, building types relatively complete, building quality good, have genealogies, inscriptions etc. documentary materials.

Of course settlement must be preserved fairly complete, old not too damaged, new not too many. However, in past half century many extremely exquisite or typical villages already destroyed; and our selection freedom very small, funding reasons, transportation reasons, even encounter intentional obstruction. We can only do our best.

Because it is a series, we尽量 avoid repetition between volumes, pay attention to each volume's characteristics. Characteristics first come from settlement itself; in topic selection, we carefully note their characteristics; in research process, we further deepen excavation.

Secondly from our writing style, not only select different angles and focuses as much as possible, even change text genre style. Some general summaries, we put in one book, other books no longer repeatedly write. As for exactly which book to write, depends on various conditions. One condition, although not main condition, is length.

Some descriptions, analyses, discussions already repeatedly seen in past residence survey reports or research papers, e.g. "suit measures to local conditions", "use local materials" etc., most readers already familiar, we no longer啰嗦. What we pursue is writing each settlement's particularity, not fitting it into generalized mold.

Only writing topic particularity, can somewhat write a bit of Chinese vernacular architecture richness and diversity. Therefore, excavating topic particularity is our research entry point, must invest considerable effort.

Excavation of typological particularity and individual particularity, also both rely on detailed application of comparative method.

Each book in this series writing time very short, because we dare not delay long on one topic, fearing here spend effort fine carving, there already destroyed how many valuable villages.

To race against destruction speed, we have to pursue speed and quantity, grab one is one; fortunately调查研究永远只能嫌少而不会嫌多。 Work somewhat superficial-simple, but we still did work seriously, we never do things carelessly.

Although we can only take a small ladle of water from the vast ocean, this ladle of water after all carries ocean's full flavor. Hope our series can arouse readers' interest in vernacular architecture, more people willing to also research them,进而 can selectively protect most valuable part, save them from complete destruction.

Chen Zhihua

Spring 1998

前言
中文

前言

我们无力回天，但我们决心用全部的精力立即抢救性地做些乡土建筑的研究工作。

——陈志华

陈老师的这句话，说得很是伤感。我似乎听到了字里行间流露出的万般无奈和苦衷。但同时，他也坚定并且气宇轩昂地宣布了"我们"的决心！

我有幸在将要走出大学校门的最后一个学期，成为"我们"中的一员，跟着陈老师、楼老师和李老师参与了两个村落的研究工作。那时乡土组的条件很艰苦，但是我和我的七个同学都觉得自己很幸运，工作也自然也很认真。在美国游荡了七年之后，我再次加入了"我们"的队伍。当我再次走进那间门上贴着狰狞门神的小屋时，我被告知过两天要"上方岩……"。

去方岩前，陈志华老师讲，"方岩有一条街、一座庙和一个书院"。

这条"街"指的就是位于方岩山脚下的岩下老街。老街形成于十九世纪中叶，在二十世纪初就已经颇具规模，到二十世纪中叶则是发展的巅峰时期。那时，街的两边排满了做香烛、食品、百货和旅店生意的店面。来来往往的香客把这条三里多长，宽度不过两三米的商业街挤得水泄不通。

从前看中国画里的奇岩绝壁，皴法皱叠，苍劲雄伟到不可思议的地步，现在到了方岩，向各山略一举目，才知道南宗北派的画山点石，都还有未到之处……

每当朝阳升起的时候，方岩山朝东的这面，也是面向老街的这一侧，便会通体映衬在暖红色的阳光下，这使原本已经是红色的山体变得更加火红耀眼。粗糙的山体又会把漫反射过来的阳光铺洒在老街上，唤醒在这里世代居住和经商的程氏后人。寿山坑里四面都是这种赤红的砂砾岩，随着日出到日落、酷暑到寒冬，山体色彩不仅会有冷暖色调的不同，而且还会附上光影的变化，更显生动。

据说峨眉山金顶上，经常会有信佛的人，在看到"佛光"的那一刹那，纵身跳崖，投身佛祖的怀抱。不难猜测，一定也曾有许多出家人从老街上化缘经过，不知当他们看到这红彤彤的山体时作何感想。至少有一位僧人，他跑到寿山坑里，利用丹岩下的山洞支木为寺；几百年后又有一个僧人，他决定上方岩修庙！所以说，没有这片丹岩丘陵，就没有日后这一座庙和一个书院。

江浙地区，山清水秀，有庙、又有书院的地方举不胜举，但是能形成像岩下老街这种规模的商业街就寥寥无几了。它们之间的关系简单地说就是，陈亮讲学及五峰书院的存在给岩下老街的发展奠定了文化的基础，而广慈寺和胡公庙旺盛的香火则是岩下老街经济发展的推动力。虽然这并没有错，但却忽视了一个奥妙所在，"庙"和"书院"的建筑，及它们每年举行的仪式性活动，都是在当地望族历代子孙的鼎立扶植之下，才能历经百年不衰。

换句话说，即使朱熹、陈亮名气再大，如果没有日后应、程、卢三姓出资，寿山坑也会随着朱熹的离去和陈亮的去世而就此沉默下去；即使胡则做了一辈子清官，被无数百姓敬仰，如果没有程氏子孙世代修缮广慈寺，胡公的香火也早就断了。因此，要真正了解岩下老街，就必须了解岩下村的望族——程氏。

岩下老街原本只是一条位于方岩山下的小路，沿路有橙麓和岩下两个自然村落。因特殊原因，岩下村在1961年被分成岩上大队和岩下大队，后改为岩上村和岩下村两个行政村落。为了避免不必要的混淆，本书沿袭传统，把岩下村和岩上村统称为岩下村。

English

Preface

"We cannot turn the tide, but we are determined to immediately devote all our energy to rescue-oriented research on vernacular architecture." — Chen Zhihua

Teacher Chen's words, spoken very sorrowfully. I seem to hear the countless helplessness and bitterness revealed between the lines. But at the same time, he also firmly and imposingly announced the determination of "us"!

I was fortunate, in my last semester before leaving university, to become one of "us", following Teacher Chen, Teacher Lou, and Teacher Li to participate in research on two villages.

At that time conditions of the vernacular architecture group were very difficult, but my seven classmates and I all felt very lucky, and work naturally also very serious. After wandering in the United States for seven years, I again joined the ranks of "us".

When I again walked into that small room with a ferocious door god pasted on the door, I was told that in two days we would "go to Fangyan..."

Before going to Fangyan, Teacher Chen Zhihua said, "Fangyan has a street, a temple, and an academy."

This "street" refers to Yanxia Old Street located at the foot of Fangyan Mountain. The old street formed in mid-19th century, already had considerable scale in early 20th century, and reached peak development in mid-20th century.

At that time, both sides of the street were lined with shops making incense candles, food, general merchandise, and inn businesses. Pilgrims coming and going crowded this commercial street over three li long and only two-three meters wide, making it impassable.

Previously looking at strange rocks and precipitous cliffs in Chinese paintings, texture methods wrinkled and layered, vigorous and majestic to incredible degree; now arriving at Fangyan, glancing at various mountains, only then know that Southern and Northern school painting mountains and dotting stones, all still have places not reached...

Whenever the sun rises, the east-facing side of Fangyan Mountain, also the side facing the old street, will be entirely set off in warm-red sunlight; this makes the originally red mountain body become even more fiery and dazzling.

The rough mountain body again spreads diffusely reflected sunlight onto the old street, awakening Cheng descendants who have lived and done business here for generations.

All four sides of Shoushan Keng are this crimson sandstone conglomerate; with sunrise to sunset, intense heat to severe winter, mountain body color not only has cool-warm tone differences, but also attaches light-shadow changes, even more vivid.

It is said that on Emei Mountain Golden Summit, often there are Buddhist believers, at the moment of seeing "Buddha Light", leap off the cliff, throwing themselves into Buddha's embrace. Not difficult to guess, there must have been many monks passing through the old street begging alms; wonder what they thought when seeing this red mountain body.

At least one monk, he ran into Shoushan Keng, using cave under cinnabar rock to support wood as temple; hundreds of years later another monk, he decided to go up Fangyan to build temple! Therefore, without this cinnabar-rock hilly land, there would be no later this temple and one academy.

In Jiangsu-Zhejiang region, beautiful mountains and clear waters, places with temples and also academies countless, but able to form commercial street of Yanxia Old Street scale are very few.

Their relationship simply said is, Chen Liang lecturing and Wufeng academy existence laid cultural foundation for Yanxia Old Street development, while Guangci temple and Hu Gong temple旺盛 incense was driving force of Yanxia Old Street economic development.

Although this not wrong, but overlooked a subtle point: "temple" and "academy" buildings, and their annual ritual activities, all under local prominent clan generations descendants' full support,才能 endure a hundred years without decline.

In other words, even if Zhu Xi, Chen Liang fame greater, without later Ying, Cheng, Lu three surnames funding, Shoushan Keng would with Zhu Xi's departure and Chen Liang's death then fall silent; even if Hu Ze served as honest official all his life, revered by countless people, without Cheng descendants generations repairing Guangci temple, Hu Gong's incense would already be cut off.

Therefore, to truly understand Yanxia Old Street, must understand Yanxia village's prominent clan — the Cheng family.

Yanxia Old Street originally was only a small road located at the foot of Fangyan Mountain; along the road there were Chengli and Yanxia two natural villages. Due to special reasons, Yanxia village in 1961 was divided into Yanshang Brigade and Yanxia Brigade, later changed to Yanshang village and Yanxia village two administrative villages.

To avoid unnecessary confusion, this book follows tradition, collectively calling Yanxia village and Yanshang village as Yanxia village.

第一章 永康的地理、历史和文化环境

Part I: Humanistic Context
1
地理位置
中文

一、地理位置

永康位于浙江省中部，原属婺州，即金华府。永康最早以地名出现是在三国吴赤乌八年（245年），当时划出乌伤县上浦乡以置永康县。在秦统一中国之前，永康及整个江浙地区属越国。公元前223年，秦灭楚，并于次年在原越国地置会稽郡。会稽郡原辖15个县，永康地区属乌伤县。

清康熙《永康县志》里有一段对当地环境及其影响的十分生动的描写：县地多山少水。故其民多重质而少权摽，好刚果而乏深沉。僻在东南，滩涩岭阻。非舟车之所辏，商贾之所聚。故民安土而不轻转徙，敦本而罕事事懋迁。壤瘠而狭，生理艰难。故其民俭啬而不竞繁华，勤苦而不甘游惰。……安居相聚，无主客之分。故耻于屈伏，激而喜争。

浙江旧时分为婺、衢、严、台、温、处、甬、绍上八府和杭、嘉、湖下三府。永康所属金华府，位于浙江中部，是各府之间联系的必经之地。永康地形属浙中丘陵盆地地区，其境内山体多为仙霞岭的余脉。仙霞岭位于浙江西南部的浙闽赣交界处，大体呈东北-西南走向，浙江境内的大盘山、天台山、会稽山等都是它的延伸，所以它又有"浙东诸山祖"的尊称。仙霞岭又是浙江两大水系，钱塘江水系和瓯江水系的分水岭。其中钱塘江水系主要位于下三府境内，也包括婺州、衢州和严州的部分地区；瓯江水系则主要位于台州、温州和处州境内。永康位于这个分水岭的西侧，和瓯江水系没有任何联系，和钱塘江水系的联系也十分脆弱。

永康中部是呈东北-西南走向的永康盆地，其中华溪自东北向西南贯穿盆地。华溪在永康旧城东北角外先后有从北面南下的酥溪、桃溪（又名北溪）并入，随后在城南与从缙云发源的南溪汇合，自此"水始胜舟"。华溪继续向西，经武义到达金华境内，最终与钱塘江水系衔接。虽然华溪在流出永康境内之前的一小段可以通舟楫，但是它是"一衣带水耳。天久不雨，则石棱锋出胶浅，且不胜一筏。是以外乡珍异之物亦罕得至者"。

所以在很长时间内，永康盆地与处于江浙地区经济中心位置的杭嘉湖平原没能建立起便利的水路往来；与经济发达的宁绍平原、港口城市温州更因为缺少水路联系而没能形成贸易往来。在铁路出现以前，货物的长距离运输主要依靠舟楫。再加上浙中丘陵众多，走陆路会有群山阻隔。所以永康是"非舟车之所辏，商贾之所聚"的地方。因此，这里没能像浙江的大部分村庄一样，自南宋起就开始大面积种植如棉花、桑麻、茶叶等经济作物，或是发展丝织、制陶、造纸等手工业，也没能形成依附于此的运输业和商业。

直到清末民初年间，永康才成为位于仙霞岭两侧城镇之间的货物转运地。那时永康有3家过塘行，分别是位于河头的"吕中和行"，章店的"协和行"，以及芝英的"广裕行"。他们将来自于钱塘江水系的草籽、茶油、豆饼、猪鬃、煤油、绸缎等货物运到永康，再用肩挑、骡马运输、车推的方式运到台、处、温州等地。同时来自于台、处、温州的木材、桐油、茶叶、棉花、草席等山货以同样的方式被运到永康，再由过塘行运到浙江其他地方。这种水路陆路混行的方式，在抗日战争初期，为国民党浙江省政府迁驻方岩，转运物资，发挥了不可替代的作用。瞬时间，永康境内的转运行，也由开始的3家一下发展到30多家。

English

1. Geographical Location

Yongkang is located in central Zhejiang Province, originally belonging to Wuzhou, i.e. Jinhua Prefecture. Yongkang first appeared as a place name in the eighth year of Chiwu of Eastern Wu (245 CE), when Shangpu Township of Wushang County was separated to establish Yongkang County.

Before Qin unified China, Yongkang and the entire Jiangsu-Zhejiang region belonged to the State of Yue. In 223 BCE, Qin conquered Chu, and the following year established Kuaiji Commandery on former Yue territory. Kuaiji Commandery originally administered 15 counties; Yongkang area belonged to Wushang County.

In the Qing Kangxi "Yongkang County Gazetteer" there is a very vivid description of local environment and its influence: The county land is mostly mountains and little water. Therefore its people are mostly substantial and lack flexibility, prefer resolute and lack depth. Remote in southeast, rapids shallow and ridges obstructed.

Not where boats and carts converge, where merchants gather. Therefore people settle on land and do not lightly migrate, esteem fundamentals and rarely engage in trade. Soil barren and narrow, livelihood difficult. Therefore its people are frugal and do not compete for prosperity, diligent and unwilling to be idle. ...

Living together peacefully, no distinction between host and guest. Therefore ashamed of submission, stirred and fond of contention.

Zhejiang in old times was divided into upper eight prefectures: Wu, Qu, Yan, Tai, Wen, Chu, Yong, Shao, and lower three prefectures: Hang, Jia, Hu. Yongkang belonged to Jinhua Prefecture, located in central Zhejiang, a necessary passage between prefectures.

Yongkang terrain belongs to central Zhejiang hilly basin region; its境内 mountains are mostly branches of Xianxia Ridge. Xianxia Ridge is located at Zhejiang-Fujian-Jiangxi border in southwestern Zhejiang, generally northeast-southwest trending; Dapan Mountain, Tiantai Mountain, Kuaiji Mountain etc.

in Zhejiang are all its extensions, so it also has the honorific title "ancestor of eastern Zhejiang mountains". Xianxia Ridge is also the watershed of Zhejiang's two major water systems: Qiantang River system and Ou River system.

Qiantang River system mainly located in lower three prefectures, also includes parts of Wuzhou, Quzhou, Yanzhou; Ou River system mainly located in Taizhou, Wenzhou, Chuzhou. Yongkang located on west side of this watershed, has no connection with Ou River system, and connection with Qiantang River system is also very fragile.

Central Yongkang is Yongkang Basin trending northeast-southwest, in which Huaxi River runs through basin from northeast to southwest.

Huaxi River outside northeast corner of Yongkang old city successively joins Suxi and Taoxi (also called Beixi) coming down from north, then converges with Nanxi originating from Jinyun at south of city, from then "water begins to support boats".

Huaxi continues west, passes Wuyi to reach Jinhua territory, finally connects with Qiantang River system. Although a small section of Huaxi before leaving Yongkang territory can pass boats, it is "a strip of water only. If no rain for long, stone edges protrude shallow, and cannot support even a raft.

Therefore rare exotic goods from other places also rarely arrive." So for a long time, Yongkang Basin and Hang-Jia-Hu Plain at economic center of Jiangsu-Zhejiang region failed to establish convenient waterway communication; with economically developed Ning-Shao Plain, port city Wenzhou, even more due to lack of waterway connection failed to form trade.

Before railways appeared, long-distance transport of goods mainly relied on boats. Plus central Zhejiang has many hills, land travel has mountain barriers. So Yongkang was a place "not where boats and carts converge, where merchants gather".

Therefore, here failed like most Zhejiang villages to start large-scale planting of cash crops such as cotton, mulberry-hemp, tea since Southern Song, or develop handicrafts such as silk weaving, pottery, papermaking, nor formed transport and commerce attached thereto.

It was not until late Qing-early Republican period that Yongkang became a cargo transshipment point between towns on both sides of Xianxia Ridge. At that time Yongkang had 3 transit firms: "Lü Zhonghe Firm" at Hetou, "Xiehe Firm" at Zhangdian, and "Guangyu Firm" at Zhiying.

They transported goods from Qiantang River system such as grass seeds, tea oil, bean cakes, pig bristles, kerosene, silk to Yongkang, then by shoulder-carrying, mule transport, cart-pushing to Tai, Chu, Wenzhou etc.

Meanwhile mountain goods from Tai, Chu, Wenzhou such as timber, tung oil, tea, cotton, straw mats were transported to Yongkang in same way, then by transit firms to other parts of Zhejiang.

This mixed water-land transport method, in early Anti-Japanese War, for Kuomintang Zhejiang provincial government relocating to Fangyan, transshipping supplies, played an irreplaceable role. In an instant, transit firms in Yongkang territory, from initial 3 suddenly developed to over 30.

本篇术语 Key Terms

永康 / Yongkang 婺州 / Wuzhou 金华府 / Jinhua Prefecture 会稽郡 / Kuaiji Commandery 乌伤县 / Wushang County 仙霞岭 / Xianxia Ridge 钱塘江水系 / Qiantang River system 瓯江水系 / Ou River system 永康盆地 / Yongkang Basin 华溪 / Huaxi River 过塘行 / Transit firm (Guotang hang) 赤乌八年 / Eighth year of Chiwu (245 CE) 永康县志 / Yongkang County Gazetteer
2
历史变迁
中文

二、历史变迁

浙江地区是越文化的发源地，也曾经是亚洲东南部"百越"文化的中心。由于交通和环境的原因，越文化和中原文化在饮食、服饰、风俗和宗教信仰等多方面都有很大区别。公元前223年，秦王灭楚之后，在原越国属地置会稽郡，开始推行汉、越两民族之间的文化融合。但是由于秦朝采取的手段强硬，再加上受长年战争的影响，会稽郡是当时全国经济和文化都十分落后的地区。

西汉时期，持续的人口增长是浙江文化和经济发展的开始。例如，在汉武帝元狩四年（公元前119年）的一次全国大规模移民过程中，据清代学者王鸣盛的估计，有十四万五千贫民迁入会稽。再加上西汉末年，又有大批北方士人、农民和手工业者逃到江南避乱。这些移民不但带来了中原的文化，还有北方地区先进的农业和手工业技术。魏晋南北朝时期，又有更多的北方人为避战乱而移民江南地区，从而使江浙地区的人口又有大幅度的增长。

接下来的隋唐盛世和大运河的开通，使南北方的经济交流和文化融合进入了新的阶段，浙江地区无论在经济方面还是文化方面都再次赶上甚至超过了北方。作为汉传佛教的重要宗派之一，天台宗于公元六世纪始创于浙江天台山。到唐朝末年，"大悲寺"在永康境内的方岩山上落成，并逐渐发展成一座天台宗名刹。

宋朝的建立标志着长年征战的结束，以及中原和江南地区文化和经济的长足发展。在北宋时期，浙江已经成为北宋王朝的经济中心，以及新儒学运动的萌芽地和重要传播地。南宋迁都临安（今杭州）后，随着大批文人墨客、士官大夫的涌入，浙江又成为全国的政治中心和文化中心。与此同时，浙江境内踊跃出的各类人才均居全国之首，其中包括宋史列传人物136人、宰相22人、词人138人、画家69人和儒者421人。难怪朱熹曾经感慨地说："岂非天旋地转，闽浙反为天地之中。

"在这种大的文化背景下，两种以反对传统经学和理学的革新思潮——事功学和心学在浙江应运而生。事功学三派均起源于浙东，分别是以吕祖谦为代表的金华学派、以叶适为代表的永嘉学派和以陈亮为代表的永康学派，而永康学派主要的讲学地点就在方岩山北边的寿山坑内。

明清两代，随着农业生产力的进一步发展，浙江部分田地转而种植收益更高的经济作物，部分劳动力也从农业劳作中解放出来，这些都为资本主义经济的萌芽创造了条件。明成化二十一年（1485年），货币税制取代了徭役制。明万历十六年（1588年），雇用人员的自由人身份在法律上得到了承认和保障。这两项变革，进一步促进了民间私营手工业和商品经济的发展。明末清初以后，方岩山上和山下老街两侧就逐渐形成了依附于广慈寺宗教活动的，制作和销售香烛供品、食品及杂货的手工业和商业；后来又发展了颇具规模的，依托于朝山进香香客的旅店业。

English

2. Historical Changes

Zhejiang region is the birthplace of Yue culture, and was once the center of "Baiyue" culture in southeastern Asia. Due to transportation and environmental reasons, Yue culture and Central Plains culture had great differences in diet, clothing, customs, religious beliefs and many other aspects.

In 223 BCE, after Qin King conquered Chu, established Kuaiji Commandery on former Yue territory, began to promote cultural integration between Han and Yue peoples. But due to Qin Dynasty's tough measures, plus influence of long-term wars, Kuaiji Commandery was a region with very backward economy and culture nationwide at that time.

Western Han period, sustained population growth was the beginning of Zhejiang cultural and economic development. For example, in a large-scale national migration in the fourth year of Yuanshou of Emperor Wu of Han (119 BCE), according to estimate of Qing scholar Wang Mingsheng, 145,000 poor people migrated into Kuaiji.

Plus late Western Han, large numbers of northern scholars, farmers and handicraftsmen fled to Jiangnan to avoid chaos. These migrants not only brought Central Plains culture, but also advanced agriculture and handicraft technology from northern regions.

Wei-Jin-Northern-Southern Dynasties period, more northerners migrated to Jiangnan to avoid war chaos, thus causing Jiangsu-Zhejiang region population to again greatly increase.

Following Sui-Tang prosperity and opening of Grand Canal, economic exchange and cultural integration between north and south entered a new stage; Zhejiang region in both economic and cultural aspects again caught up with or even surpassed the north.

As one of important sects of Chinese Buddhism, Tiantai sect was founded in sixth century CE at Tiantai Mountain in Zhejiang. By late Tang Dynasty, "Dabei temple" was completed on Fangyan Mountain in Yongkang territory, and gradually developed into a famous Tiantai sect temple.

Establishment of Song dynasty marked end of long-term warfare, and considerable development of culture and economy in Central Plains and Jiangnan regions. In Northern Song period, Zhejiang had already become economic center of Northern Song dynasty, as well as birthplace and important dissemination ground of Neo-Confucianism movement.

After Southern Song moved capital to Lin'an (present Hangzhou), with influx of large numbers of literati, scholar-officials, Zhejiang again became national political center and cultural center.

At the same time, various talents emerging in Zhejiang ranked first nationwide, including 136 biographical figures in Song History, 22 prime ministers, 138 ci poets, 69 painters, and 421 Confucian scholars.

No wonder Zhu Xi once emotionally said: "Is it not that heaven and earth rotated, Min-Zhe反而 became center of heaven and earth." Under this great cultural background, two innovative trends opposing traditional classical studies and Neo-Confucianism — Utilitarianism and Philosophy of Mind — emerged in Zhejiang.

Three schools of Utilitarianism all originated in eastern Zhejiang: respectively Jinhua school represented by Lü Zuqian, Yongjia school represented by Ye Shi, and Yongkang school represented by Chen Liang; and Yongkang school's main lecturing location was in Shoushan Keng north of Fangyan Mountain.

Ming-Qing two dynasties, with further development of agricultural productivity, part of Zhejiang farmland turned to planting higher-yield cash crops, part of labor also freed from agricultural work; these all created conditions for sprouts of capitalist economy.

Twenty-first year of Chenghua of Ming (1485), monetary tax system replaced corvée system. Sixteenth year of Wanli of Ming (1588), free person status of hired workers legally recognized and guaranteed. These two reforms further promoted private handicraft and commodity economy development.

After late Ming-early Qing, on Fangyan Mountain and both sides of old street at foot, gradually formed handicraft and commerce attached to Guangci temple religious activities, making and selling incense candles offerings, food and groceries; later developed considerable-scale inn industry relying on mountain-pilgrimage worshippers.

本篇术语 Key Terms

越文化 / Yue culture 百越 / Baiyue (Hundred Yue) 天台宗 / Tiantai sect 大悲寺 / Dabei Temple 新儒学 / Neo-Confucianism 事功学 / Utilitarianism (Shigong xue) 永康学派 / Yongkang school 金华学派 / Jinhua school 永嘉学派 / Yongjia school 陈亮 / Chen Liang 吕祖谦 / Lü Zuqian 叶适 / Ye Shi 朱熹 / Zhu Xi 大运河 / Grand Canal 货币税制 / Monetary tax system

文化特点
中文

三、文化特点

回顾浙江的历史可以看到，由于受越文化的影响，浙江地区的文化自古以来就有信鬼敬神和重利事功的特点。越人信仰的"鬼神"多为福祥之神，其中又以水神最为重要。对水神的祭祀不仅包括"春祭三江，秋祭五湖"，还以龙舟和龙灯的形式表现出来。这是因为自古以来人们就认为龙"春分而登天，秋分而潜渊"，并且可以招云降雨。再加上春分到秋分之间的雨水，对于农作物生产，特别是江浙地区水稻的种植有至关重要的作用。这种对鬼神的崇拜，经过与中原文化的融合，便逐渐转化为对佛教、道教的泛神信仰和各种民间神祇的普遍存在。

在明正德年间（1506—1521年）编写的《永康县志》风俗篇里，当地风俗特点第二条写的就是"信鬼神，重淫祀"，在下面还注着"出汉志"三个字。而在清康熙三十七年（1698年）编写的《永康县志》中，依然记载着在民间葬礼上不用僧道而用巫师的"陋俗"。

重利事功观念的形成，究其本源是因为江浙地区生活环境曾经十分恶劣，土地分散窄小且不利耕种，这便促使了早期商品经济的发展。现在人们用"计然之策"来泛指生财致富之道。计然，又作计倪，是春秋时期越王勾践的一位谋士。他的良策既包括一些从经验和现状出发，居安思危的思想，又包括一些商品经济的理论。到了南宋，即浙江经济发展的巅峰时期，强调"各务其实"的陈亮创立的永康学派，提出了"义利双行"的观点，毅然站在了中国传统儒学和经学的对立面上。

他的思想，以及同属事功学派的金华学派和永嘉学派，却都在江浙地区得到了广泛的支持与传播。思想上的解放提高了商人的地位，在浙江诸葛村的宗谱里，还记载有鼓励子孙从商的族训。商人的增加和商品经济的发展，使重利事功的思想更加根深蒂固。同样在明正德《永康县志》里，保留了自宋志中就有提及的"急于进取，善于图利"的当地民风。岩下老街一边是香火旺盛、佛道合一的方岩山，一边是家家户户做生意的程氏后人。信鬼敬神和重利事功的思想，不仅是老街文化形成的原因，并且渗透在当地文化的点滴之中。

English

3. Cultural Characteristics

Reviewing Zhejiang history can see, due to influence of Yue culture, Zhejiang region culture since ancient times has characteristics of believing in ghosts and revering gods, and valuing profit and utilitarian achievement. "Ghosts and gods" believed by Yue people were mostly auspicious gods, among which water god most important.

sacrifice to water god not only includes "spring sacrifice to three rivers, autumn sacrifice to five lakes", but also expressed in form of dragon boats and dragon lanterns. This is because since ancient times people believed dragon "ascends to heaven at spring equinox, dives into abyss at autumn equinox", and can summon clouds and rain.

Plus rainfall between spring and autumn equinoxes has crucial role for crop production, especially rice cultivation in Jiangsu-Zhejiang region. This worship of ghosts and gods, through integration with Central Plains culture, gradually transformed into pantheistic belief in Buddhism, Daoism and universal existence of various folk deities.

In "Yongkang County Gazetteer" customs chapter compiled during Ming Zhengde period (1506-1521), second item of local customs characteristics wrote "believe in ghosts and gods, value excessive sacrifices", below also noted three characters "from Han History".

And in "Yongkang County Gazetteer" compiled in 37th year of Qing Kangxi (1698), still recorded "vulgar custom" of using shamans instead of Buddhist/Daoist priests at folk funerals.

formation of valuing-profit-utilitarian concept, tracing its origin, is because Jiangsu-Zhejiang region living environment was once very harsh, land scattered narrow and unfavorable for cultivation, this promoted early commodity economy development. Now people use "Ji Ran's strategies" to generally refer to ways of making wealth.

Ji Ran, also called Ji Ni, was a counselor of King Goujian of Yue in Spring and Autumn period. His good strategies included both some thoughts starting from experience and current situation, being vigilant in peace time, and some commodity economy theories. By Southern Song, i.e.

peak period of Zhejiang economic development, Chen Liang who emphasized "each attends to its reality" founded Yongkang school, proposed viewpoint of "righteousness and profit both practiced", resolutely stood on opposite side of traditional Chinese Confucianism and classical studies.

His thought, as well as Jinhua school and Yongjia school also belonging to utilitarian school, all received wide support and dissemination in Jiangsu-Zhejiang region. Ideological emancipation raised merchants' status; in genealogy of Zhuge village in Zhejiang, also recorded clan instructions encouraging descendants to engage in commerce.

Increase of merchants and development of commodity economy made valuing-profit-utilitarian thought even more deeply rooted. Similarly in Ming Zhengde "Yongkang County Gazetteer", preserved local folk custom "eager for advancement, skilled at seeking profit" already mentioned since Song gazetteer.

Yanxia Old Street on one side is Fangyan Mountain with旺盛 incense, Buddhism-Daoism unity; on other side are Cheng descendants every household doing business. Believing in ghosts-revering gods and valuing-profit-utilitarian thought, not only are reasons for old street culture formation, but also permeate into every bit of local culture.

本篇术语 Key Terms

信鬼敬神 / Believing in ghosts and revering gods 重利事功 / Valuing profit and utilitarian achievement 水神 / Water god 龙舟 / Dragon boat 龙灯 / Dragon lantern 泛神信仰 / Pantheistic belief 淫祀 / Excessive sacrifices (Yinsi) 巫师 / Shaman 计然之策 / Ji Ran's strategies 勾践 / King Goujian of Yue 义利双行 / Righteousness and profit both practiced 诸葛村 / Zhuge Village 宗谱 / Genealogy (Zongpu) 族训 / Clan instructions

第二章 岩下老街的发展

Part II: Planning
1
影响岩下老街历史发展的几个重要因素
中文

一、影响岩下老街历史发展的几个重要因素

在江浙地区，像岩下村这样依附在丘陵地带河谷中的小村庄可能有成千上万个。如果没有接下来这几件事情的发生，岩下村可能就会像其他小村庄一样默默无闻下去，甚至有可能在历史的长河中逐渐消失。幸运的是，正是这几件看起来相对独立，甚至在当时可能也不是什么大不了的事，才成就了日后的岩下老街，以及它独特的历史和文化。而要讲这几件事，还要从传说开始谈起。

从五峰的传说开始

相传这里很早以前是一片平地。孙悟空在蟠桃大会上随手扔了几个仙桃到人间，便长成了一片桃林。后来就有了一女四男五位年轻人，住进这里照看这片桃林。他们在这里生活到老，并共同演绎了各种版本的浪漫爱情故事。最后，他们一起变成了五座山峰。为了纪念这五位先人，人们就用这五位先人的特点依次给这五座山峰命名，并将它们统称为寿山，也叫桃岩。

位于北面并有天然石洞，"较诸峰为淳固深厚"的叫固厚峰；固厚峰西面挂有瀑布的叫瀑布峰；瀑布峰东南是覆釜峰，故顾名思义它像一口倒扣的铁锅；再向东南，"岩色赤白相间，状类桃花"的叫桃花峰；最南面的因能听到鸡鸣声而叫鸡鸣峰。这五座山峰，峰峰相连，只在东面留了一个山口。而中间被围合的有山洞、有瀑布、有桃花、并可闻鸡鸣的山谷，则宛若人间仙境、世外桃源。也正因为如此，这里日后便成了许多历史人物的舞台和一些重要事件的发生地，同时也成就了一系列关于其他山峰的传说。

最有趣的一个就是有一个蜈蚣精想霸占五峰，导致四面受敌的故事。由此便有了以蜈蚣山为中心，周围一系列山峰的得名。蜈蚣山位于五峰东边山谷的东侧，它的西面是鸡笼山，即方岩山，南面为蜥蜴山，北面是狮子山，西北边则是象鼻岗。

寿山寺的创建

在方岩地区的历史上，第一件重要的事发生在南北朝时期的梁普通元年（520年），这年寿山寺创建。寿山寺初名桃岩寺，位于五峰之一固厚峰下的石洞内，它是永康县境内最早创立的寺院。寿山寺作为一个佛教寺院的意义并不显著，但是它的重要性在于它为日后朱熹、陈亮等南宋学者提供了读书和讲学的场所，它同时也是明朝创建的五峰书院和清朝修建的胡则家庙的前身，它更是在抗日战争时期为国民党浙江省政府提供了临时的办公场所。

火头和尚建大悲寺

在唐宣宗大中四年（850年），一个来自于浙江天台山国清寺，法号为"正德"的火头和尚，在方岩山顶上创建了大悲寺，即广慈寺的前身。大悲寺在一千多年间坎坷而富有戏剧性的经历，特别是它和当地胡、程两大家族之间的纷杂关系，都对岩下村的发展有着至关重要的影响。

进士胡则

在宋朝统一中国并恢复科举制度之后，永康县胡库人胡则在北宋端拱二年（989年）进士及第，成为婺州地区首位进士。他在北宋年间为官四十七年，"逮事三朝，十握州符，六持使节"。由于他奏免衢州、婺州两地身丁钱，而深受当地百姓爱戴。在他死后，对他的祭祀活动始于方岩山上广慈寺的读书堂，后遍布衢、婺两州。同时，他也得到了宋朝五位皇帝的八次赐封，并被当地百姓尊称为"胡公大帝"。对胡则的祭祀和崇拜不仅遍及江浙地区，而且远到江西、福建等地，并发展成为一种极具地方特色的民俗文化。这种民间祭祀活动，更是对岩下村的发展有决定性的作用。

状元陈亮

公元1127年，宋高宗举国南渡并把都城迁到了离永康仅二百五十里远的临安。在接下来的一百多年里，永康境内的文化氛围达到了鼎盛。在绍熙四年（1193年），年已五十一岁的陈亮金榜题名中了状元，成为永康历史上第二位，也是最后一位状元。而在这之前，陈亮已经在寿山寺内讲学十几年，并成为"永康学派"的创始人。在南宋时期江浙地区经济飞速发展的大环境下，陈亮提出了"商籍农而立，农赖商而行"的颇具资本主义萌芽的思想，打破了中国自古以来的"重农抑商"的传统。

在这个思想的进一步推动下，永康的社会和经济在南宋以后有了长足的发展。特别是商业和手工业更是比周边地区更加兴盛发达，因此素有"百工之乡"的美称。岩下村更是利用自身文化和地域的优势，发展起了其独特的旅馆和百货业。

槐塘程氏的迁入

如果以上这些都属天时地利的外在原因的话，槐塘程氏的迁入即最终成就了岩下村独特的历史和文化。永康程氏的始迁祖程锴于十四世纪初，携子从安徽歙县槐塘，经金华迁到永康衙后巷。几十年后，他的两个孙子又分别迁到了方岩山下的橙麓村和岩下村。程锴原本来自书香世家，他本人也是至元二十年（1283年）进士并曾任湖广廉访司副使。程氏的子孙则是本着"重文教礼义德行"和"士农工商各宜习业"的家训，在方岩山下生息繁衍了七百多年。是程氏的后人，把岩下村最终变成了一个极具特点的血缘村落。

English

1. Several Important Factors Influencing the Historical Development of Yanxia Old Street

In Jiangsu-Zhejiang region, small villages like Yanxia village attached to hilly river valleys may number in thousands. If the following events had not occurred, Yanxia village might have remained obscure like other small villages, or even gradually disappeared in the long river of history.

Fortunately, it was these seemingly relatively independent events, perhaps not significant at the time, that achieved later Yanxia Old Street and its unique history and culture. And to tell these events, we must start from legends.

Starting from the Legend of Five Peaks

Legend has it that long ago here was a flat land. Sun Wukong casually threw several peaches of immortality to the human world at the Peach Banquet, which grew into a peach forest. Later there were five young people, one woman and four men, who moved in to take care of this peach forest.

They lived here until old age, and together performed various versions of romantic love stories. Finally, they together turned into five mountain peaks. To commemorate these five ancestors, people named the five peaks in sequence according to their characteristics, and collectively called them Shoushan, also called Taoyan.

Located in the north with natural stone cave, "more solid and profound than other peaks" is called Guhou Peak; west of Guhou Peak with waterfall is called Waterfall Peak; southeast of Waterfall Peak is Fufu Peak, as its name suggests it looks like an upside-down iron pot; further southeast, "rock color red-white alternating, shaped like peach blossom" is called Peach Blossom Peak; southernmost because can hear rooster crowing is called Jiming (Rooster Crow) Peak.

These five peaks, peak connected to peak, only leave a mountain pass in the east. And the valley enclosed in the middle with caves, waterfalls, peach blossoms, and audible rooster crowing, is like a fairyland on earth, a paradise beyond the world.

Precisely because of this, here later became the stage for many historical figures and the location of some important events, and also achieved a series of legends about other peaks. The most interesting one is the story of a centipede spirit wanting to occupy Five Peaks, leading to being attacked from all sides.

From this came the naming of a series of peaks around Centipede Mountain as the center. Centipede Mountain is located east of the valley east of Five Peaks; its west is Jilong Mountain, i.e. Fangyan Mountain; south is Lizard Mountain; north is Lion Mountain; northwest is Elephant Trunk Hill.

Founding of Shoushan temple

In Fangyan region history, the first important event occurred in the Northern and Southern Dynasties period, the first year of Putong of Liang (520 CE), when Shoushan temple was founded. Shoushan temple initially named Taoyan temple, located in the stone cave under Guhou Peak, one of Five Peaks; it was the earliest temple founded in Yongkang County.

Shoushan temple's significance as a Buddhist temple is not notable, but its importance lies in that it provided a place for reading and lecturing for later Southern Song scholars such as Zhu Xi, Chen Liang; it was also the predecessor of Wufeng academy founded in Ming dynasty and Hu Ze family temple built in Qing dynasty; it更是 provided temporary office space for Kuomintang Zhejiang provincial government during Anti-Japanese War.

Fire-Head Monk Builds Dabei temple

In the fourth year of Dazhong of Emperor Xuanzong of Tang (850 CE), a fire-head monk from Guoqing temple on Tiantai Mountain in Zhejiang, with dharma name "Zhengde", founded Dabei temple on top of Fangyan Mountain, i.e. the predecessor of Guangci temple.

Dabei temple's rough and dramatic experience over more than a thousand years, especially its complex relationship with local Hu and Cheng two major clans, all had crucial influence on Yanxia village development.

jinshi Hu Ze

After Song dynasty unified China and restored imperial examination system, Hu Ze from Huku, Yongkang County, passed jinshi examination in the second year of Duangong of Northern Song (989 CE), becoming the first jinshi in Wuzhou region.

He served as official for forty-seven years in Northern Song, "served three dynasties, held ten prefectural seals, six envoy credentials". Because he petitioned to exempt personal head tax in Quzhou and Wuzhou, he was deeply loved by local people.

After his death, sacrificial activities for him began at the reading hall of Guangci temple on Fangyan Mountain, later spread throughout Qu and Wu prefectures. At the same time, he also received eight bestowals from five Song emperors, and was respectfully called "Emperor Hu Gong" by local people.

sacrifice and worship of Hu Ze not only spread throughout Jiangsu-Zhejiang region, but also as far as Jiangxi, Fujian etc., and developed into a folk culture with strong local characteristics. This folk sacrificial activity更是 had decisive effect on Yanxia village development.

Zhuangyuan Chen Liang

In 1127 CE, Emperor Gaozong of Song moved the entire state south and relocated capital to Lin'an, only two hundred fifty li from Yongkang. In the following more than one hundred years, cultural atmosphere in Yongkang reached its peak.

In the fourth year of Shaoxi (1193 CE), Chen Liang, already fifty-one years old, passed the zhuangyuan (first place) examination, becoming the second and last zhuangyuan in Yongkang history. Before this, Chen Liang had already lectured in Shoushan temple for over ten years, and became founder of "Yongkang School".

Under the great environment of rapid economic development in Jiangsu-Zhejiang region during Southern Song, Chen Liang proposed the thought "commerce relies on agriculture to stand, agriculture relies on commerce to act", quite with capitalist sprout characteristics, breaking China's ancient tradition of "valuing agriculture and suppressing commerce".

Under further promotion of this thought, Yongkang society and economy had considerable development after Southern Song. Especially commerce and handicraft were more prosperous than surrounding areas, therefore known as "hometown of all crafts".

Yanxia village更是 utilized its own cultural and geographical advantages, developed its unique inn and general merchandise industries.

Migration of Huaitang Cheng clan

If above all belong to external reasons of favorable timing and location, the migration of Huaitang Cheng clan ultimately achieved Yanxia village's unique history and culture. Yongkang Cheng clan's first ancestor Cheng Kai in early 14th century, took his son from Huaitang, She County, Anhui, via Jinhua to Yongkang Yahou Lane.

Decades later, his two grandsons respectively moved to Chengli village and Yanxia village at the foot of Fangyan Mountain. Cheng Kai originally came from a scholarly family; he himself was also a jinshi in the twentieth year of Zhiyuan (1283 CE) and once served as deputy commissioner of Huguang Lianfangsi.

Cheng descendants, based on family instructions "valuing culture, education, propriety, righteousness and virtue" and "scholar, farmer, artisan, merchant each should practice their profession", lived and multiplied at the foot of Fangyan Mountain for over seven hundred years.

It was Cheng descendants who ultimately turned Yanxia village into a highly distinctive consanguineous village.

老街的地理环境位置与自然环境
中文

二、老街的地理环境位置与自然环境

在明、清时期，永康分为十个乡、四十七都、一百一十二图。游仙乡内三十四都和三十五都所辖范围，缘其境内的方岩山而又被统称为方岩。方岩位于永康盆地东缘，其境内基本包括两组大体呈东北-西南走向的山丘，其地理环境可以用"七山二水一分田"来概括。在这两组低山之间有许多更为低矮的丘陵，它们共同围合成了一条南北走向五里多长的山谷。山谷里有华溪的支流，方岩溪自南向北蜿蜒流过。

岩下老街原本就是山谷之中、方岩溪畔的一条小路，路边的村庄因对面拔地而起的方岩山而取名为"岩下"。当这条小路的两边都盖满了房屋和商店时，路就变成了"街"，自然得名为"岩下街"。由于这条山谷只在北面有一个出口，出去以后向南二十多里可以到永康县城，向北则接东阳。与地处永康盆地内的其他村庄相比，岩下老街的位置交通更为闭塞。

由于这里的丘陵主要是由红色砂砾岩构成，而且十分陡峭，不能开垦梯田，因此这条山谷便成为村里的主要耕地。岩下老街紧贴着位于山谷的东侧一组低矮丘陵，由北向南分别是狮子山、蜈蚣山和蜥蜴山，相对较高的方岩山和象鼻岗则位于山谷西侧。方岩山脚相对较为平缓，又有一些植被，因此又被当地人称作坟山，用以埋葬先人。

这些丘陵之间又形成了几个大体呈西北-东南走向的山坑，坑中多有溪水顺地势流向山谷并汇入方岩溪。狮子山北面是较为开阔的割稻坑；狮子山和蜈蚣山之间是长一里多、宽度不过百米的下胡坑；再往南，就是总面积稍小一点儿的，位于蜈蚣山和蜥蜴山之间的上坑；蜥蜴山南边的斜坑，就是整个山谷的南端。这些山坑里还拥有村里另一部分耕地。

在南方丘陵地区，由于受到地形的限制，村落中的住宅很难以祖屋或祠堂为中心向外发展。它们多是顺应地势，沿河谷或依山脚而建。这样做最主要的原因是要把最好的土地留作耕地；同时，离山近也方便打柴，从山上流下来的溪水在这里也是最干净的；再有就是如果遇到洪水，这里地势相对较高，也会比较安全。老街途经的两个村庄，在选址上都是符合这个常理的，但是也有一些独到的地方。由于整条山谷南北走向，两侧的丘陵西高东低，所以山谷里的田地从早上到下午都可以得到充足的日照。

高度较高的方岩山位于山谷西侧，对于村落中的住宅来讲，它不但可以遮挡住夏天日落前燥热的西晒，还可以挡住冬天的冷风。另一边相对较低的狮子山、蜈蚣山和蜥蜴山，还可以保证山坑里的庄稼也能沐浴在阳光里。

岩下老街全长约三里，宽度仅二三米，中间原为青石板铺路，边用鹅卵石镶嵌，与建筑台明相接的地方还做有排水沟。老街除了南边的一小段向西拐了两拐并结束于方岩山的入口之外，其余部分基本上是南北走向，并且一路都沿着山根儿和坑口。老街在狮子山北边的一小段属于橙麓村，橙麓村是以割稻坑为中心发展起来的。在坑口南侧，方岩溪几乎以垂直的角度从老街下穿过，转而平行老街继续向北。这几米长的石板路被称作"松门桥"。过桥向南，便属于岩下村了。岩下村是以上坑为起点，沿老街向南向北发展。

在蜥蜴山南侧，老街向西拐的地方，方岩溪又一次从老街下面穿过。这一次，横跨方岩溪的是一个名曰"回龙桥"的小石拱桥，这也是过去岩下村南边的村界。在石拱桥东边的石柱上还写着"处州方岩大路"几个字，意为沿路继续向南就可以到达处州。从清光绪《永康县志》中收录的永康道里图来看，官路到方岩就结束了，而连接处州缙云县的大路则要经过方岩南面的峰昆岭。由此看来，这可能仅是条小路，或者可能会辗转接到山南边的那条缙云大路上。

English

2. Geographical Location and Natural Environment of the Old Street

In Ming and Qing periods, Yongkang was divided into ten townships, forty-seven du, one hundred twelve tu. The jurisdiction of 34th du and 35th du in Youxian Township, because of Fangyan Mountain in its territory, was also collectively called Fangyan.

Fangyan is located at eastern edge of Yongkang Basin; its territory basically includes two groups of hills generally trending northeast-southwest; its geographical environment can be summarized as "seven mountains, two waters, one field".

Between these two groups of low mountains there are many lower hills, which together enclose a north-south trending valley over five li long. In the valley there is a tributary of Huaxi River, Fangyan Stream winding from south to north.

Yanxia Old Street was originally a small path in the valley, beside Fangyan Stream; the village by the road was named "Yanxia" (Under Rock) because of Fangyan Mountain rising abruptly opposite. When both sides of this path were covered with houses and shops, the road became a "street", naturally named "Yanjia Street".

Because this valley only has an exit in the north, after going out southward over twenty li can reach Yongkang county seat, northward connects to Dongyang. Compared with other villages located in Yongkang Basin, Yanxia Old Street's location transportation is more isolated.

Because the hills here are mainly composed of red sandstone conglomerate, and very steep, cannot be terraced, therefore this valley became the village's main arable land.

Yanxia Old Street closely follows a group of low hills on the east side of the valley, from north to south respectively Lion Mountain, Centipede Mountain and Lizard Mountain; relatively higher Fangyan Mountain and Elephant Trunk Hill are located on the west side of the valley.

Fangyan Mountain foot is relatively gentle, with some vegetation, therefore also called Grave Mountain by locals, used for burying ancestors.

Between these hills又 formed several mountain ravines generally trending northwest-southeast; in ravines there are mostly streams flowing along terrain into valley and merging into Fangyan Stream.

North of Lion Mountain is relatively open Gedao (Rice Cutting) Ravine; between Lion Mountain and Centipede Mountain is Xiahu Ravine over one li long and no more than one hundred meters wide; further south is Shang (Upper) Ravine with slightly smaller total area, located between Centipede Mountain and Lizard Mountain; Xie (Slanting) Ravine south of Lizard Mountain is the southern end of the entire valley.

These ravines also contain another part of the village's arable land.

In southern hilly regions, due to terrain limitations, village residences很难 develop outward centered on ancestral house or ancestral hall. They mostly follow terrain, built along river valleys or at mountain foot.

The main reason for this is to leave best land as arable land; at the same time, being close to mountains also facilitates firewood gathering; streams flowing down from mountains here are also cleanest; furthermore, if encountering floods, here terrain is relatively high, also safer.

The two villages the old street passes through, in site selection both conform to this common sense, but also have some unique aspects. Because the entire valley trends north-south, hills on both sides are west-high east-low, so fields in the valley can get sufficient sunlight from morning to afternoon.

The higher Fangyan Mountain is located on the west side of the valley; for village residences, it not only can block the hot western sun before sunset in summer, but also can block cold wind in winter. The other side relatively lower Lion Mountain, Centipede Mountain and Lizard Mountain, can also ensure crops in ravines bathe in sunlight.

Yanxia Old Street total length about three li, width only two-three meters; middle originally paved with bluestone slabs, edges inlaid with cobblestones; where connecting with building platform bases there are drainage ditches.

Except for a small southern section that turns west twice and ends at the entrance of Fangyan Mountain, the rest basically trends north-south, and all along follows mountain foot and ravine mouths. A small section of the old street north of Lion Mountain belongs to Chengli village; Chengli village developed centered on Gedao Ravine.

South of the ravine mouth, Fangyan Stream almost perpendicularly passes under the old street, then turns parallel to the old street continuing north. These few meters of stone slab road are called "Songmen bridge". Crossing the bridge southward, belongs to Yanxia village.

Yanxia village started from Shang Ravine, developed southward and northward along the old street. On the south side of Lizard Mountain, where the old street turns west, Fangyan Stream again passes under the old street.

This time, spanning Fangyan Stream is a small stone arch bridge called "Huilong (Returning Dragon) bridge", which was also the southern village boundary of Yanxia village in the past. On the stone pillar east of the arch bridge are also written several characters "Chuzhou Fangyan Main Road", meaning continuing south along the road can reach Chuzhou.

From Yongkang road distance map included in Qing Guangxu "Yongkang County Gazetteer", official road ended at Fangyan, while the main road connecting Jinyun County of Chuzhou had to pass through Fengkun Ridge south of Fangyan. From this, this may only be a small path, or may connect via detours to the Jinyun main road south of the mountain.

本篇术语 Key Terms

方岩溪 / Fangyan Stream 七山二水一分田 / Seven mountains, two waters, one field 红色砂砾岩 / Red sandstone conglomerate 割稻坑 / Gedao Ravine (Rice Cutting Ravine) 下胡坑 / Xiahu Ravine 上坑 / Shang Ravine (Upper Ravine) 斜坑 / Xie Ravine (Slanting Ravine) 青石板铺路 / Bluestone slab paving 鹅卵石镶嵌 / Cobblestone inlay 松门桥 / Songmen Bridge 回龙桥 / Huilong Bridge (Returning Dragon Bridge) 小石拱桥 / Small stone arch bridge 台明 / Platform base (Taiming) 西晒 / Western sun exposure

槐塘程氏的迁入和村落的早期状况
中文

三、槐塘程氏的迁入和村落的早期状况（十四世纪中叶到十五世纪中叶）

目前岩下村和橙麓村的居民，除极少数外，余下均姓程。世远公宗谱记载，永康程氏的祖先可以追溯到公元前九世纪周宣王的时代，而程氏第一本家谱的编纂也早在西汉年间。到东晋年间，第四十五世祖元谭公因任新安（今徽州）太守，由于他"有德于民，民留不得还"，从而成为程氏南迁始祖。自第三十世祖延坚公起，程氏便在安徽歙县槐塘居住。在槐塘居住的程氏已经发展为望族，程氏子孙"多以文章发身，超擢显爵，遂开基筑室，分上、下、正、旧四府居之"。下府程锴在元至元二十年（1283年）中进士，初任监察御史，后升为湖广廉访司副使。

在延佑元年（1314年），程锴"奉命平云南寇，因被诬戍辰州（今湖南），后携子益宁避地金华石苍岭"。七年后，即1320年，程锴易名为楷，并带全家移居永康县城衙后巷。到元至正十三年（1353年），程益宁的儿子都已长大成人，程家便一分为三。长子闰一公程木留居衙后巷；次子闰二公程杉迁到了如今的橙麓村；闰三公程权则迁到了不远的岩下村。依据家谱中的记载，迁居岩下的程氏在几代之内都还保持了这种长子留居原籍，余下子孙迁去异乡的迁居方式。

程权，字可与（1333—1385年），别号得耕居士。宗谱中收录有《得耕公传》，其中阐述到他厌倦城市里的喧嚣，于是搬到方岩山东边居住，并"建楼匾得耕，曰，终身得耕陇亩足矣"，并由此得名为"得耕居士"。这幢住宅的原址应该位于目前得耕居的东侧，即更靠近山根儿的地方。程权想做一个隐士，但是他却生活在一个动荡的年代里。在他35岁的时候，明朝建立了。他认为"国初为官者朝不保夕"，从而拒绝从军参政。他为此先是断指，最终服毒自尽以求子孙日后平安。程权的子孙果然没有辜负他的一片苦心，程氏家族也在日后多代人的不断努力下日渐兴旺。

程权的夫人在他去世后三年也撒手人寰，撇下了两个幼子——鹏和洋。等他们长大以后，依惯例，程鹏留岩下，程洋迁居上呈。程鹏有三个长到成年的儿子——祚、禧和裕，分家之后他们分别做了天、地、人房的房祖。在明宣德（1426—1435年）初年，祖屋里不能再容下程家这三代十几口人了。于是在一番寻找之下，地房迁附东，后易村名为下宅；人房迁前刘；天房程祚（1392—1458年），字永延，带着当时还年幼的次子程垣和幼子程坚迁往凌宅，后易村名为独松；程祚的长子程坦（1411—1477年），字世远，一人留居岩下。

English

3. Migration of Huaitang Cheng clan and Early village Conditions (Mid-14th to Mid-15th Century)

Currently residents of Yanxia village and Chengli village, except very few, the rest all surnamed Cheng. Shiyuan Gong genealogy records, Yongkang Cheng clan ancestors can be traced to the era of King Xuan of Zhou in 9th century BCE, and compilation of Cheng clan's first genealogy was as early as Western Han period.

By Eastern Jin period, 45th ancestor Yuantan Gong because served as governor of Xin'an (present Huizhou), due to his "virtue to people, people detained him and he could not return", thus became the Cheng clan's southern migration first ancestor. From 30th ancestor Yanjian Gong onward, Cheng clan lived in Huaitang, She County, Anhui.

Cheng clan living in Huaitang had already developed into a prominent clan; Cheng descendants "mostly rose through literature, promoted to high ranks, then established bases and built houses, divided into Upper, Lower, Principal, Old four mansions to live".

Lower mansion Cheng Kai passed jinshi in the twentieth year of Zhiyuan of Yuan (1283 CE), initially served as investigating censor, later promoted to deputy commissioner of Huguang Lianfangsi.

In the first year of Yanyou (1314 CE), Cheng Kai "ordered to pacify Yunnan bandits, because falsely accused was exiled to Chenzhou (present Hunan), later took son Yining to take refuge at Shicangling, Jinhua". Seven years later, i.e. 1320, Cheng Kai changed name to Kai, and moved whole family to Yahou Lane, Yongkang county seat.

By the thirteenth year of Zhizheng of Yuan (1353 CE), Cheng Yining's sons had all grown up, Cheng family divided into three. Eldest son Runyi Gong Cheng Mu stayed at Yahou Lane; second son Runer Gong Cheng Shan moved to present Chengli village; Runsan Gong Cheng Quan moved to not-far Yanxia village.

According to genealogy records, Cheng clan who migrated to Yanxia within several generations still maintained this migration method of eldest son staying in original home, remaining descendants migrating to foreign lands.

Cheng Quan, courtesy name Keyu (1333-1385), alternative name Degeng Jushi (Retired Scholar of Obtaining Plowing).

Genealogy includes "Biography of Degeng Gong", which states he tired of city noise, so moved to live east of Fangyan Mountain, and "built building with plaque Degeng, saying, lifelong able to plow fields is enough", and thus named "Degeng Jushi". The original site of this residence should be located east of present Degeng residence, i.e.

closer to mountain foot. Cheng Quan wanted to be a hermit, but he lived in a turbulent era. At age 35, Ming dynasty was established. He believed "officials at dynasty founding cannot guarantee from morning to evening", thus refused to join army or participate in politics.

For this he first cut off finger, ultimately committed suicide by poison to seek descendants' future peace. Cheng Quan's descendants indeed did not disappoint his painstaking efforts; Cheng clan also gradually prospered under continuous efforts of many later generations.

Cheng Quan's wife also passed away three years after his death, leaving two young sons — Peng and Yang. When they grew up, by convention, Cheng Peng stayed at Yanxia, Cheng Yang migrated to Shangcheng.

Cheng Peng had three sons who reached adulthood — Zuo, Xi and Yu; after family division they respectively became ancestors of Heaven, Earth, Human branches. In early Ming Xuande (1426-1435), the ancestral house could no longer accommodate Cheng family's three generations of over ten people.

So after some searching, Earth branch moved to Fudong, later changed village name to Xiazhai; Human branch moved to Qianliu; Heaven branch Cheng Zuo (1392-1458), courtesy name Yongyan, took then-young second son Cheng Yuan and youngest son Cheng Jian to Lingzhai, later changed village name to Dusong; Cheng Zuo's eldest son Cheng Tan (1411-1477), courtesy name Shiyuan, alone stayed at Yanxia.

本篇术语 Key Terms

程权 / Cheng Quan 得耕居士 / Degeng Jushi 得耕居 / Degeng Residence 程鹏 / Cheng Peng 程祚 / Cheng Zuo 程坦 / Cheng Tan 世远公 / Shiyuan Gong 天房 / Heaven branch (Tianfang) 地房 / Earth branch (Difang) 人房 / Human branch (Renfang) 独松 / Dusong 衙后巷 / Yahou Lane 新安 / Xin'an (Huizhou) 元谭公 / Yuantan Gong
4
程氏的壮大和村落的初步发展
中文

四、程氏的壮大和村落的初步发展（十五世纪中叶到十八世纪末）

从三世祖程权到六世祖程坦这四代人期间，岩下村还是一个杂姓聚居的小村落，这一点可以从永康地区程氏各房派的家谱中找到线索。对于每个程氏子孙迎娶来的新娘，家谱中都会记载新娘的姓氏和原籍。从十四世纪中叶到十五世纪中叶，即程氏迁到永康的前一百年里，新娘还有不少是来自岩下村的，特别又以胡、任和陈姓居多，但是这种现象在十五世纪中叶以后便逐渐减少。

岩下村当时应该是位于上坑口内、蜈蚣山南麓、上坑溪以北、老街东侧的这块"背山面水，坐北朝南"的风水宝地。目前在得耕居东面，有十几幢散落分布的小型住宅，而且多是解放后建造的。据村里的老人讲，这里以前有三座大院子。从现在散落分布的村落布局中还能依稀勾勒出以前院落的平面。导致这种现状的原因，很有可能是因为这些院落的建筑年代较早，在"土改"分房时分到各家的房屋建筑状况已经不是很好，于是各家分别就地重建的结果。

程氏三世祖程权建造的有匾为"得耕"的宅院，位于现得耕居前面水塘的东侧，即靠近蜈蚣山的地方。这个院子可能也不是很大，村里当时或许也没有什么空地可以建更大的新房。以至于在宣德（1426—1435年）初年，程氏宁愿在别村买地建房，也不愿或不能留在岩下村。当时村里的人口并不多，所以山谷里和山坑里的耕地应该能足以养活村里的人口，不然三世祖程权也不可能欣然于"终身得耕陇亩"的状态。相比住在永康其他地方的程氏，迁来岩下的这支人丁并不兴旺。但可能正因为如此，才为岩下程氏以后的发展奠定了一定的物质基础。

四世祖程鹏曾"施广慈寺土田二十余亩，供奉忠佑侯胡公"；在明永乐乙酉年（1405年），他又"输纳东北一区税粮数百石不取贷。造飞桥，设义浆，济人往来"。又例如，在明宣德乙卯年（1435年）和正统乙丑年（1445年）当地歉收，五世祖程祚以"吾家颇饶，乃忍坐视其死"为初衷，替乡里缴纳数百石的官粮。家谱中类似的记载难免会有夸张的成分，即便如此，也可以看出岩下程氏在当时也是衣食无忧的大户人家。

岩下村的程氏，从第七世程鑅到第十四世，是一个逐渐壮大和发展的过程。六世祖程坦留居岩下后，一直膝下无子。年过花甲之后，便过继了住在独松的二弟程坚的第十一个儿子程鑅（1474—1548年）。不料三年之后，程坦就去世了，年仅三岁的程鑅便成了程家在岩下村唯一的男丁。家谱中记载他"童年立家，人称练达，好礼重义，应事刚果，许人以诺，终不渝"。经过大半生的努力，他到晚年便在老屋的西侧建造了一个呈半月形的水池，池边筑亭，并在池后建了新居，这就是现在得耕居的前身。

据《世远公宗谱》中的描述，池底有用卵石镶嵌成"双龙戏珠"的图案。建筑外围台门砖雕"双狮抢球"，门额砖刻"得耕居"，并配花鸟浮雕。建筑内部为前厅后堂，其装饰之华美，以至于有人状告衙门说是程家私造宫廷。程鑅也因每日消遣在这位于方岩山东边的亭台里，而被称为"东亭居士"。程鑅在独松村的侄子，礼部侍郎程文德（1497—1559年）还亲笔题写了"树德堂"及楹联"栋宇初成家有庆，簪缨世显国同休"挂在前厅。程文德日后还深刻评论了得耕堂中的"得耕"二字的深远含义。

他这样写道："禹稷躬稼，文王即田，功诗咏豳风，礼著耕籍，咸重耕者也。耕者生民之本，衣食之原也。得耕则以勤而兴，不得耕则逸而亡。"有了这座大房子，再加上"得耕"和"树德"这四个字，程氏后人就在岩下站稳了脚跟，并逐渐发展成为望族。程鑅有两子六孙，两个儿子分为松、柏两房，六个孙子再次分做福、禄、寿和日、月、星六房。由于日、月、星三房的人口一直不多，所以他们一直被统称为柏房。和以往不同的是，这四房人谁也没有再迁出岩下。

在这之后的一百多年里，岩下村的程氏都过着平平淡淡的生活。正如家谱里记载的一样，他们每个人一生中值得记下的事情无非是出生、死亡、娶妻和生子。但是他们却逐渐在岩下村拥有了更多的房屋和土地。到了第十三世，禄房下出了一个作勳公（1646—1725年），名际烈。《世远公宗谱》中把他描述为"性情敦朴，节俭持躬，铢积寸累以成家业。而资雄里闬，每遇岁歉必出粟赈饥，凡有求者缓急靡不相济。人皆德之"。郡侯张公也敬仰他的德行，并赠匾曰"熙朝逸叟"。作勳公不但儿孙满堂，而且他们也都得到村里人的尊敬。

其长子伊臣"文艺精通"，并且独修宗谱；次子经臣"善承家业"。直到今天，作勳公及其子孙还都是村里人津津乐道的话题。老人们都会掰着手指，兴致勃勃地讲：作勳公有两个儿子，友常和恭常；友常有四个儿子，叫琴、棋、书、画；恭常也有四个儿子，为发、强、刚、毅。村里人提到的这些字，都是房派的名字。虽然从家谱上看，友常，即伊臣只有三个儿子，所以琴、棋、书、画可能只是反映了人们对他们所尊敬的人的一种完美的希冀。村里的两座重要祠堂，世远公祠和作勳公祠应该就是在这期间先后建成的。

从两个祠堂的位置可以看出，程氏在岩下拥有的房产和地产，又向西推进了一大块。岩下程氏已经俨然步入了当地望族之列，那时向他们提亲的也不乏附近一些官宦人家。

在程氏家族逐步壮大的过程中，岩下村也随之发展起来，并逐渐演变成为一个程姓血缘村落。村落的建设在这一时期越过了老街的界限，向西面地势较低的山谷地带发展，并以方岩溪作为新的村落边界。据村里的老人讲，在方岩溪、老街和蜈蚣山并到一起的地方，曾经有一座门，也被称为"城头"。它标识了岩下村北面的入口，城头外面当时则是一望无际的田地。

十六世纪初，在陈亮曾经讲学的寿山坑内相继落成了丽泽祠和丽泽精舍，后易名为五峰书院。明末，又在丽泽祠的西侧建了学易斋。这里便成了学者云集的地方，仅明朝在五峰书院研习的进士就有三十多人。曾在这里主讲的学者有三位是永康程氏后人，他们分别是独松村的吏部左侍郎程文德（1497—1559年）、文楼村的程梓和程梓之子四川左布政使程正谊。岩下村的程氏虽然没有人在寿山讲学，但是鉴于五峰书院仅在三里之外，去听的或许不在少数。

况且当时岩下村七世祖程鑅是从独松村过继来的，即程文德的叔叔，所以这两个村的程氏一向相互往来密切，程文德难免会把他在五峰书院结交的朋友带到岩下小坐。《世远公宗谱》中就有记载，程文德在程鑅建的"池亭设宴招待明学者礼部尚书黄绾"。

与此同时，方岩山上的广慈寺也在逐渐发展壮大。特别是在十七世纪中叶，当胡公像被移入大殿供奉之后，广慈寺的香火变得更加兴旺。岩下老街是上山的必经之路，无论是慕名而来的香客，或是每逢初一、十五从下属寺院回来述职的住持，还是每年清明上方岩山祭祖的文楼村程方峰的后人，都要从岩下村穿过。当时，大部分需要住宿的香客都是借宿在广慈寺的僧房或大殿里。但是，在十七世纪中叶以后，文楼村的程氏可能就已经开始在天街上做一些蜡烛和供品的生意。不难想象，和文楼程氏同宗的岩下程氏，也可能在正月和八月农闲的时候，也就是香火最旺的时候，在自家门口做些茶水、小吃或当地特产之类的小买卖。

English

4. Growth of Cheng clan and Initial village Development (Mid-15th to Late 18th Century)

During the four generations from 3rd ancestor Cheng Quan to 6th ancestor Cheng Tan, Yanxia village was still a small village with mixed surnames; this can find clues in genealogies of various Cheng branch factions in Yongkang region. For each bride married to Cheng descendants, genealogy records the bride's surname and original home.

From mid-14th to mid-15th century, i.e. the first hundred years after Cheng clan migrated to Yongkang, many brides still came from Yanxia village, especially Hu, Ren and Chen surnames predominated, but this phenomenon gradually decreased after mid-15th century.

Yanxia village at that time should be located on this geomantically precious land "backing mountain facing water, sitting north facing south" within Shang Ravine mouth, south foot of Centipede Mountain, north of Shang Ravine Stream, east of old street.

Currently east of Degeng residence, there are over ten scattered small residences, mostly built after liberation. According to village elders, there used to be three large courtyards here. From current scattered village layout can still vaguely outline former courtyard plans.

The reason for this current situation is likely because these courtyards were built earlier, during "land reform" housing distribution the building conditions of houses allocated to each family were not very good, so each family separately rebuilt in place.

The courtyard with plaque "Degeng" built by 3rd ancestor Cheng Quan, located east of the pond in front of present Degeng residence, i.e. near Centipede Mountain. This courtyard may also not be very large; the village at that time perhaps also had no vacant land to build larger new houses.

So much so that in early Xuande (1426-1435), Cheng clan preferred to buy land and build houses in other villages, rather than or could not stay in Yanxia village.

At that time village population was not large, so arable land in valley and ravines should be sufficient to support village population; otherwise 3rd ancestor Cheng Quan could not have been content with "lifelong able to plow fields" state.

Compared with Cheng clan living in other parts of Yongkang, this branch that migrated to Yanxia was not populous. But perhaps precisely because of this, it laid a certain material foundation for later development of Yanxia Cheng clan.

4th ancestor Cheng Peng once "donated over twenty mu of temple land to Guangci temple, for worshipping Zhongyou Hou Hu Gong"; in Ming Yongle Yiyou year (1405), he又 "paid several hundred shi of tax grain in northeast district without taking loan. Built flying bridge, set free porridge, helped travelers".

Another example, in Ming Xuande Yimao year (1435) and Zhengtong Yichou year (1445) local crop failure, 5th ancestor Cheng Zuo with original intention "my family is fairly well-off, how can I bear to sit and watch them die", paid several hundred shi of official grain for the village.

Similar records in genealogy inevitably have exaggerated elements; even so, can see Yanxia Cheng clan at that time was also a well-off family with no worries about food and clothing.

Yanxia village Cheng clan, from 7th generation Cheng Heng to 14th generation, was a process of gradual growth and development. After 6th ancestor Cheng Tan stayed at Yanxia, he remained childless. Over sixty years old, he adopted the eleventh son Cheng Heng (1474-1548) of his second brother Cheng Jian living in Dusong.

Unexpectedly three years later, Cheng Tan died; only three-year-old Cheng Heng became the only male of Cheng family in Yanxia village. Genealogy records he "established family in childhood, people called him experienced, valued propriety and righteousness, handled affairs decisively, promised others and never broke promise".

Through most of his life's efforts, in old age he built a crescent-shaped pond on the west side of old house, built pavilion by pond, and built new residence behind pond; this is the predecessor of present Degeng residence.

According to description in "Shiyuan Gong Genealogy", pond bottom has cobblestone inlaid pattern of "two dragons playing with pearl". Building outer gate brick-carved "two lions grabbing ball", gate lintel brick-carved "Degeng residence", with bird-flower relief.

Building interior is front hall rear hall; its decoration was so magnificent that someone sued to the magistrate claiming Cheng family privately built palace. Cheng Heng also because daily消遣 in this pavilion east of Fangyan Mountain, was called "Dongting Jushi (East Pavilion Scholar)".

Cheng Heng's nephew in Dusong village, Vice Minister of Rites Cheng Wende (1497-1559), also personally wrote "Shude Hall" and couplet "building newly completed family has celebration, official generations prominent nation shares peace" hanging in front hall.

Cheng Wende later also profoundly commented on the profound meaning of the two characters "Degeng" in Degeng Hall. He wrote: "Yu and Ji personally farmed, King Wen farmed, poems praise Bin style, rites record plowing, all value plowing. Plowing is the root of people's livelihood, the source of food and clothing.

Obtaining plowing then prospers through diligence, not obtaining plowing then perishes through idleness." With this big house, plus the four characters "Degeng" and "Shude", Cheng descendants established a firm foothold in Yanxia, and gradually developed into a prominent clan.

Cheng Heng had two sons six grandsons; two sons divided into Song (Pine) and Bai (Cypress) two branches; six grandsons again divided into Fu (Blessing), Lu (Salary), Shou (Longevity) and Ri (Sun), Yue (Moon), Xing (Star) six branches. Because Ri, Yue, Xing three branches population always not large, they were always collectively called Bai branch.

Different from before, none of these four branches migrated out of Yanxia.

In the following more than one hundred years, Yanxia village Cheng clan all lived ordinary lives. Just as genealogy records, what each person worth recording in lifetime was nothing more than birth, death, marriage and childbirth. But they gradually owned more houses and land in Yanxia village.

By 13th generation, under Lu branch emerged Zuoxun Gong (1646-1725), named Jilie. "Shiyuan Gong Genealogy" describes him as "disposition honest and simple, thrifty in personal conduct, accumulated bit by bit to establish family business.

And wealthy in village, every year of poor harvest必定 gave grain to relieve famine, anyone in need regardless of urgency all helped. People all praised his virtue." Prefect Zhang also respected his virtue, and gifted plaque "Recluse of Prosperous Dynasty".

Zuoxun Gong not only had grandchildren full hall, but they also all received village people's respect. His eldest son Yichen "skilled in literature and arts", and alone repaired genealogy; second son Jingchen "good at inheriting family business". Until today, Zuoxun Gong and his descendants are still topics village people relish.

Elders all count on fingers, enthusiastically tell: Zuoxun Gong had two sons, Youchang and Gongchang; Youchang had four sons, called Qin (Lute), Qi (Chess), Shu (Calligraphy), Hua (painting); Gongchang also had four sons, Fa (Develop), Qiang (Strong), Gang (Firm), Yi (Persevering).

These characters village people mention are all branch faction names. Although from genealogy, Youchang, i.e. Yichen only had three sons, so Qin, Qi, Shu, Hua may only reflect people's perfect hope for those they respect.

The village's two important ancestral halls, Shiyuan Gong Hall and Zuoxun Gong Hall, should have been built successively during this period. From positions of the two halls can see, Cheng clan's houses and land in Yanxia,又 advanced westward by a large block.

Yanxia Cheng clan had already stepped into local prominent clan ranks; at that time those proposing marriage to them included some nearby official families.

In the process of Cheng clan's gradual growth, Yanxia village also developed accordingly, and gradually evolved into a Cheng-surname consanguineous village. village construction in this period crossed the old street boundary, developed westward to lower valley terrain, and used Fangyan Stream as new village boundary.

According to village elders, where Fangyan Stream, old street and Centipede Mountain converge, there used to be a gate, also called "Chengtou" (City Head). It marked the north entrance of Yanxia village; outside Chengtou at that time was endless fields.

Early 16th century, in Shoushan Keng where Chen Liang once lectured, successively completed Lize temple and Lize Jingshe, later renamed Wufeng academy. Late Ming,又 built Xueyi Studio west of Lize temple. Here became a gathering place for scholars; only in Ming dynasty there were over thirty jinshi who studied at Wufeng academy.

Three scholars who once lectured here were Yongkang Cheng descendants: respectively Left Vice Minister of Personnel Cheng Wende (1497-1559) of Dusong village, Cheng Zi of Wenlou village, and Cheng Zi's son Left Administrative Commissioner of Sichuan Cheng Zhengyi.

Although no one from Yanxia village Cheng clan lectured at Shoushan, but given Wufeng academy only three li away, those who went to listen may be not few. Moreover at that time Yanxia village 7th ancestor Cheng Heng was adopted from Dusong village, i.e.

Cheng Wende's uncle, so Cheng clan of these two villages always had close mutual exchanges; Cheng Wende inevitably would bring friends he made at Wufeng academy to Yanxia for a short visit. "Shiyuan Gong Genealogy" has record, Cheng Wende "held banquet at pond pavilion built by Cheng Heng to entertain Ming scholar Minister of Rites Huang Wan".

At the same time, Guangci temple on Fangyan Mountain also gradually grew. Especially in mid-17th century, when Hu Gong statue was moved into main hall for worship, Guangci temple's incense became even more prosperous.

Yanxia Old Street was the only way up the mountain; whether pilgrims coming for fame, or abbots returning from subordinate temples on first and fifteenth of each month to report duties, or Wenlou village Cheng Fangfeng's descendants going up Fangyan Mountain to worship ancestors every Qingming, all had to pass through Yanxia village.

At that time, most pilgrims needing accommodation stayed in monk rooms or main hall of Guangci temple. But after mid-17th century, Wenlou village Cheng clan may have already started doing some candle and offering business on Heavenly Street.

Not difficult to imagine, Yanxia Cheng clan of same clan as Wenlou Cheng, may also in first and eighth months slack farming season, i.e. when incense most prosperous, do small business of tea, snacks or local specialties at their own doorsteps.

5
程氏的鼎盛和村落的线性发展
中文

五、程氏的鼎盛和村落的线性发展（十八世纪末到二十世纪中叶）

岩下程氏自十五世纪起，便进入了发展的鼎盛时期。这其中最重要的人物就是作勳公的孙子，程经臣的小儿子程立重（1736—1793年）。程立重字逢时，号毅庵，是毅房的房祖。关于这个字"逢时"的程氏后人，还有一个被称为"岩脚竖花厅，丈人倒贴银"的美丽爱情故事。相传程立重自小就和住在芝英紫霄观，知县应炜公的女儿订了婚。这个生于官宦人家的小千金，从小就备受宠爱。在她十三岁的一天，家人为了寻找她丢失的毽子，竟无意中发现了埋在家里的十八桶白银。应炜公认为这是女儿的缘分，就决定将这十八桶白银留给女儿长大后做嫁妆。

不仅如此，应炜公还怕女儿嫁出去吃苦，于是在过门之前，派人到岩下村为女儿建好了一座三厅两堂的三进宅院，院子大到可以容下"屋里塘"和"屋里井"。程立重为了报答丈人赠银之恩，在乾隆五十三年（1788年），不惜重金在新居西边造了雕饰精美的绍常祠堂。

岩下村北面的村口原本是村落发展的一个天然边界。出村口向北的一段距离内，方岩溪和老街之间的空间太小，不足以容下一个院落。不仅如此，老街随山势逐渐爬坡，和山谷中的田地形成了一定的高差，这更增加了建造的难度。但是，程立重及其后人在短短几十年中持续的建设，使程氏的家业不可能仅局限在下坑口附近，而是不得不开始沿老街向南、向北，在山谷里寻求发展。

程立重宅院位于下坑口的南面，院落西墙紧贴着方岩溪，东墙外则是蜥蜴山，老街被卡在建筑和山体之间。绍常祠堂位于这座新宅的西侧，即方岩溪的西岸。在嘉庆二年（1797年），程立重的后人在村口以北又建造了一座新的院落，后来被称为"义丰雅记"。从村落建筑布局以及山势、道路和河流之间的相互关系推断，这几幢建筑当时应该都是建在原有耕地之上。对已有耕地的占用，说明了人口的增长。而人口持续增长和耕地逐渐减少之间的矛盾，会进一步加速其他非农业经济模式的形成和发展。

程立重宅院和义丰雅记在对地形的处理上还有一些相同点。老街的地平是起起伏伏的。通常来说，靠近坑口的一段比较低；紧贴山麓的部分则比较高，最高的地方可能和方岩溪有将近一层的高差。程立重宅院和义丰雅记的选址都位于老街和方岩溪有一定高差的地方。它们的处理方法都是采用不规则形状的石料围做墙基，中间用夯土填平，这样把院落抬到和老街同高。在这两个院落的西面，方岩溪几乎都是紧贴着院墙向北流，并在院落南北两侧各有一个将近九十度的转弯。从这一点可以推断出，为了建造这两座住宅，人们可能还对河道进行了相应的修整。

在十九世纪的前几十年里，又有不少建筑相继落成。紧贴义丰雅记北面和南面各有一座宅院先后落成，它们前后连通，构成了一组被称为"风、雅、颂"的三进院落。在狮子山和蜈蚣山之间的下胡坑坑口，禄常祠堂、程正伟火腿店、新隆兴旅店还有经堂沿老街西侧一字排开。

由于这些新院落的建成，村落的范围进一步向北扩展。对村落有保卫作用，同时也是用来"拦风水"的"城头"，也被移到了下胡坑坑口的北边。它沿着下胡坑溪的北岸，横跨狮子山和方岩山之间的山谷。城头是一堵约两丈宽、一丈高的土墙，墙头种有四季常青的樟树。每年正月十三，当闹龙灯的队伍走过城头的时候，龙头和龙尾还要在这里嬉戏一番，当地叫"跳灯"。

在岩下程氏不断壮大和村落建设持续发展的同时，广慈寺的香客也越来越多，于是山上的僧房可能就不够住了。不仅如此，在道光二十九年（1849年），一把大火烧毁了广慈寺的大部分建筑，迫使寺院在一年多后才得以重建。于是在道光年间（1821—1850年），岩下村有记载的第一家旅社开业了，店号程隆兴。这条旅社位于村口以南，老街以西，是进村以后看到的第一座大宅院。

广慈寺虽然作为一方名刹，但是在大火以后，它便开始走向衰落。再加上清末国运不济，鸦片泛滥，广慈寺的许多僧房都被当地老百姓拆掉卖钱。但是当地和周边地区的百姓对胡公的崇拜并没有因此而消退，这种日渐虔诚的祭祀活动进一步促进了岩下村建筑和经济的发展。

在十九世纪末二十世纪初，又有近十座院落在下胡坑坑口附近建成。这些院落大多两层高，它们一幢紧挨一幢地排列在老街的东西两侧，局部还有两层高的封闭走廊横跨老街，连接两侧的院落。对于仅两三米宽，曲曲折折，而且并不平坦的老街来说，这些院落所围合的空间，不仅尺度宜人，而且在热闹的时候又能烘托气氛。

当下胡坑坑口已经没有新宅院的落脚之地的时候，有几家大的旅社先后在松门桥以北，老街西侧开张。这里属橙麓村，更接近山谷的入口，也就是做旅店生意最有利的地方。在二十世纪中叶以前，又有许多小的旅社、商店及住宅陆陆续续地建在了下胡坑和上坑之间，松门桥和下胡坑之间，还有橙麓村内的老街两侧。值得一提的是，在下胡坑和上坑之间还有一个很小的，没有名字的山坑。由于在坑口外，老街东侧坐落着属于三兄弟的三幢小型院落，所以这里被称作"三间头"。在坑口附近还相对集中地散落着几家中小型的旅社。

岩下村沿老街向北发展的同时，也在同步的向南延伸，并在二十世纪初达到高潮。只是老街在经过程立重宅院不远后便向西拐了两拐，并结束于方岩山的入口，所以这里的发展空间并不大，大的院落不多。再加上这里是老街的尽端，并不是做旅店生意最有利的位置，这里的店面主要经营方岩货，兼卖食品，并以外姓居多。据程茂发老人回忆，这里的高姓和周姓人家主要做雨伞，王姓人家则做笋干。

到二十世纪中叶，这条三里多长的商业街就最终形成了。由于地形和历史的原因，它的每一段都有自己的特点。它的北段位于割稻坑口，以几家大型的旅店作为开端；接着的一段紧贴狮子山的西麓，由一些相对散落的中小型的旅店和杂货店组成；到下胡坑坑口附近，则是有几家超大型的豪华旅店摩肩接踵地矗立在老街两侧；再往南，蜈蚣山西侧的一小段也只有几家不太集中的旅店和杂货店；紧接着上坑口的一段，由于人口密集，空房不多，各家只是用临街的房子经营些小吃和方岩货之类的小买卖；最南端的一段位于蜥蜴山以西，主要以方岩货为主，并兼营食品。

在正二月和八九月的进香期，两三米宽的街道会被挤得水泄不通。街上不仅有远道而来的香客，还有挑着扁担的手艺人和路边摆摊的小商贩。他们会经营诸如熏豆腐、肉麦饼、橘子、菱角和小五金等小买卖。就连橙麓村年近古稀的程名同老人讲，每逢进香期不单是老街上，就连橙麓村也会被挤得水泄不通。这期间，伴随香客们远道而来的还有许多真真假假的乞丐，游历于老街附近和上方岩的途中。

English

5. Peak of Cheng clan and Linear Development of village (Late 18th to Mid-20th Century)

Yanxia Cheng clan since 15th century entered peak development period. The most important figure among them was Zuoxun Gong's grandson, Cheng Jingchen's youngest son Cheng Lizhong (1736-1793). Cheng Lizhong courtesy name Fengshi, hao Yi'an, was ancestor of Yi branch.

About this Cheng descendant with courtesy name "Fengshi" (Meeting the Time), there is also a beautiful love story called "at rock foot erect flower hall, father-in-law subsidizes silver". Legend has it Cheng Lizhong since childhood was engaged to daughter of County Magistrate Ying Wei Gong, living in Zhiying Zixiao temple.

This young lady born in official family, since childhood was deeply favored. One day at age thirteen, family searching for her lost shuttlecock, unexpectedly discovered eighteen barrels of silver buried at home. Ying Wei Gong believed this was daughter's destiny, decided to leave these eighteen barrels of silver for daughter's dowry when grown.

Not only that, Ying Wei Gong also feared daughter would suffer after marriage, so before she moved in, sent people to Yanxia village to build for daughter a three-courtyard residence with three halls and two main halls; courtyard large enough to contain "indoor pond" and "indoor well".

Cheng Lizhong to repay father-in-law's gift of silver, in the fifty-third year of Qianlong (1788), spared no expense to build exquisitely carved Shaochang ancestral hall west of new residence.

The north village entrance of Yanxia village was originally a natural boundary for village development. Within a distance north from village entrance, space between Fangyan Stream and old street was too small to accommodate a courtyard.

Moreover, old street gradually climbs with mountain terrain, forming a certain height difference with fields in valley, this further increased construction difficulty.

However, Cheng Lizhong and his descendants' continuous construction in just a few decades, made Cheng family property impossible to be limited only near Xia Ravine mouth, but had to start along old street southward and northward, seeking development in the valley.

Cheng Lizhong residence is located south of Xia Ravine mouth; courtyard west wall closely follows Fangyan Stream, outside east wall is Lizard Mountain, old street is wedged between building and mountain. Shaochang ancestral hall is located west of this new residence, i.e. west bank of Fangyan Stream.

In the second year of Jiaqing (1797), Cheng Lizhong's descendants built another new courtyard north of village entrance, later called "Yifeng Yaji". From village building layout and mutual relationships between mountain terrain, roads and rivers infer, these buildings at that time should all have been built on original arable land.

Occupation of existing arable land illustrates population growth. And contradiction between continuous population growth and gradual decrease of arable land, will further accelerate formation and development of other non-agricultural economic models.

Cheng Lizhong residence and Yifeng Yaji also have some similarities in terrain treatment. Old street ground level is undulating. Generally speaking, section near ravine mouth is relatively low; part close to mountain foot is relatively high, highest place may have nearly one-story height difference with Fangyan Stream.

Cheng Lizhong residence and Yifeng Yaji both selected sites where old street and Fangyan Stream have certain height difference. Their treatment method both uses irregular-shaped stone to enclose wall foundation, middle filled with rammed earth, thus raising courtyard to same height as old street.

West of these two courtyards, Fangyan Stream almost both closely follows courtyard wall flowing north, and on north and south sides of courtyard each has a nearly ninety-degree turn. From this can infer, to build these two residences, people may also have correspondingly modified the river channel.

In first decades of 19th century, many buildings were completed successively. Close to north and south of Yifeng Yaji each had a residence completed successively; they connect front and back, forming a group of three-courtyard residences called "Feng (Wind), Ya (Elegance), Song (Ode)".

At Xiahu Ravine mouth between Lion Mountain and Centipede Mountain, Luchang ancestral hall, Cheng Zhengwei Ham shop, Xinlongxing Inn and Jingtang line up along west side of old street.

Due to completion of these new courtyards, village scope further expanded northward. "Chengtou" (City Head), which had defensive function for village and also used to "block fengshui", was also moved to north of Xiahu Ravine mouth. It follows north bank of Xiahu Ravine Stream, spanning the valley between Lion Mountain and Fangyan Mountain.

Chengtou is an earthen wall about two zhang wide and one zhang high; wall top planted with evergreen camphor trees. Every year on 13th of first lunar month, when dragon lantern procession passes through Chengtou, dragon head and dragon tail also play here for a while, locally called "Tiaodeng" (Lantern Jumping).

While Yanxia Cheng clan continuously grew and village construction持续 developed, Guangci temple's pilgrims also increased, so monk rooms on mountain may not have been enough. Moreover, in the twenty-ninth year of Daoguang (1849), a big fire burned most of Guangci temple buildings, forcing temple to rebuild only after over a year.

So in Daoguang period (1821-1850), Yanxia village's first recorded inn opened, shop name Cheng Longxing. This inn is located south of village entrance, west of old street, is the first large courtyard seen after entering village.

Guangci temple although as a famous local temple, but after the big fire, it began to decline. Plus late Qing national fortune poor, opium rampant, many monk rooms of Guangci temple were demolished by local people for money.

But local and surrounding area people's worship of Hu Gong did not therefore subside; this increasingly devout sacrificial activity further promoted Yanxia village building and economic development.

In late 19th-early 20th century, nearly ten more courtyards were built near Xiahu Ravine mouth. These courtyards mostly two stories high; they stand one next to another on east and west sides of old street; in parts there are two-story high enclosed corridors spanning old street, connecting courtyards on both sides.

For the old street only two-three meters wide, winding, and not flat, the space enclosed by these courtyards, not only has pleasant scale, but also can enhance atmosphere when busy.

When Xiahu Ravine mouth already had no space for new courtyards, several large inns successively opened north of Songmen bridge, west of old street. Here belongs to Chengli village, closer to valley entrance, i.e. most favorable location for inn business.

Before mid-20th century, many small inns, shops and residences were successively built between Xiahu Ravine and Shang Ravine, between Songmen bridge and Xiahu Ravine, and on both sides of old street within Chengli village. Worth mentioning, between Xiahu Ravine and Shang Ravine there is also a very small, unnamed mountain ravine.

Because outside ravine mouth, east side of old street sits three small courtyards belonging to three brothers, so here is called "Sanjiantou" (Three Heads). Near ravine mouth also relatively concentrated scattered several small and medium inns.

While Yanxia village developed northward along old street, it also simultaneously extended southward, and reached climax in early 20th century. Only the old street after passing Cheng Lizhong residence not far then turns west twice, and ends at entrance of Fangyan Mountain, so development space here not large, big courtyards few.

Plus here is the end of old street, not most favorable location for inn business; shops here mainly sell Fangyan goods, also sell food, and mostly non-Cheng surnames. According to elderly Cheng Maofa's recollection, Gao and Zhou surname families here mainly made umbrellas, Wang surname families made dried bamboo shoots.

By mid-20th century, this over three-li commercial street was finally formed. Due to terrain and historical reasons, each section has its own characteristics.
